Algorithm ideas:
1. Build good initial sequence using low-dimensional "block" optimization:
   - Choose block count K (e.g., 64 or 128) and samples per block L = n//K (or continuous mapping). For each K, run optimization over block heights h (positive) to minimize score of the expanded sequence length n. Instead of expanding and FFT each iteration (n large), for small K we can compute exact convolution of expanded blocks efficiently as block convolution with counts. But easier: choose n_block=K (length K) and optimize over K; then expand to n. But K small score worse. Better optimize expansion directly using FFT for n=4096; K=64, each iteration FFT 4096 ~fast. We can make 10k iterations maybe seconds. Good.

   We can do continuous mirror descent over h (not each n element). Let a = repeat_interleave(h, L) scaled to sum1 (or max=1). Compute q=conv(a,a). Score. Gradient w.r.t h: since a is constant over blocks, objective smooth. We can compute gradient on a and average over blocks, or derive block gradient. Then update h positive via multiplicative gradient. Since dimension K small, easy. Use adaptive step, restarts.

   This helps global shape. We can also randomly initialize h as exponential, sinusoidal, random, etc.

2. Full-sequence refinement:
   - Normalize sequence to sum=1 (or scale max=1). Use gradient of smoothed max with all peaks or selected top peaks via FFT.
   - Use multiplicative/exponential gradient updates (natural parameter x) or projected gradient.
   - Periodically enforce positivity and scale; use clipping/line search.

Could combine: Start from a simple structured sequence (maybe h from block optimization) and then full refinement. Also try random restarts with different block K.

Need ensure below 1.502. Let's think what level block optimization can achieve. We can perhaps analytically solve large K using polynomial? Might be enough to just output a known sequence like K=64 optimized at runtime. But without execution, need design algorithm likely works.

Alternative: Use scipy.optimize.minimize on block heights with custom objective (score) and Powell/Nelder-Mead? But scipy may be less reliable. Custom mirror descent more controllable.

Let's design low-dimensional block optimizer in detail:
- n fixed (e.g., parent length 6962 or 8192). K = e.g. 64. L = n // K, remap first rem blocks get L+1. Or easier use a = np.repeat(h, L) and trim to n. If n not divisible, slight discontinuities but okay.
- h positive. Objective score = 2n max(convh) / sum(h_or_a)^2. Since scaling invariant, set sum(a)=1 after each block update. Actually if a = normalized, values ~1/n. For FFT, conv max ~1/n. Use sum=1 for numerical.
- Fast self convolution via rfft. n up to 8192, fft length next pow2 ~16384. Very fast ~0.2ms? Python overhead maybe 1ms, 10k iterations 10s.
- Block gradient: F soft or max.
   Use smooth log-sum-exp of q peak? Since q is nonnegative. On sum=1, max q around maybe 1/(n*?) ~1e-5 for n=1e4 with score1.5 => q~7.5e-8? Wait qmax=score/(2n) ~1.5/20000=7.5e-8. Smaller than average 1/(2n)=5e-8. Double precision can handle.
   We can use adaptive top gradient as before, but over h. For soft: β weights = softmax(β q) / sum; compute gradient a = 2 cross_correlation(a, w). Then average gradient over blocks to h_grad.
   Since h dimension small, can also do finite-difference? No.

- Update h using multiplicative mirror: logh -= step * h_grad (since F gradient wrt a? Need account normalization). Simpler: work with a full and use full multiplicative, but restrict to block by averaging and repeating? We can do:
   - Compute grad_a for objective (unconstrained sum). For mirror descent on a positive with fixed sum, update a ∝ a exp(-η grad_a). If a is block-constant, after update it won't be exactly block-constant if grad varies within block? But grad_a from objective will vary within block because convolution depends only on full positions; even if a constant over block, grad may vary near block boundaries due to convolution? It may be identical within a block? It depends on position relative to peaks; likely varies, so update would break block structure. We can average update multiplicatively per block: h_new = h * exp(-η grad_block_avg) / normalized. This is like coordinate-wise mirror for block variables. Not exact gradient descent for h if objective not block-constant? Since objective as function of h is same regardless of distribution within block if a exactly block-constant. Let's decide final algorithm components:
A. Candidate generators:
   - Uniform length n (score2) as fallback.
   - Blocks: K levels, random/geometric/sine init, optimized with block mirror descent.
   - Fourier ansatz: p_i = (1+ρ z_i) normalized? Use random basis? Maybe not necessary if full sequence optimizer from block inits.

   - Use fixed sum=1. Fast FFT q. Softmax/adaptive top peaks. Mirror/exponential gradient with adaptive step.
   - Periodically do random perturbations and block-based restarts to escape.
   - Use global timeout 2300s. Return best.

Adaptive line search for mirror step:
```
def mirror_update(p, grad, eta):
    p_new = p * np.exp(-eta * (grad - grad.mean()))  # grad mean maybe zero
    s = p_new.sum()
    return p_new / s
```

Full optimizer function:

def optimize_sequence(p, end_time, rng, topk, gamma, init_step, patience):
    n=len(p); p=p/sum
    q=fast_self_conv(p); score=...
    best=p.copy(); no_imp=0
    step=init_step
    gamma_cur=gamma
    while time<end:
       # compute weights grad
       G, M, qmax = weighted_grad(...)
       # normalize gradient maybe
       gn = np.linalg.norm(G) / np.sqrt(n)  # RMS
       if gn<1e-14: break
       Gn = G / gn  # dimensionless
       # try line search around step
       improved=False
       for scale in [step, step*0.5, step*0.25, step*0.1]:
           p2 = p * np.exp(-scale * Gn)
           if p2.sum()<1e-300: continue
           p2/=p2.sum()
           q2=fast_self_conv(p2); s2=score
           if s2<best_score - tol: accept; break
       if not improved:
           # random perturb or reduce step
```

def refine(p0, end_time, rng, topk=128, gamma=20, lr=0.05, patience=200):

`weighted_gradient(p,q,k,gamma)`:
```
n=len(p); q_l=2*n-1
if k>=q_l: k=q_l
# top indices
if k > 1:
   idx=np.argpartition(q, -k)[-k:]
else: idx=np.argmax(q)
vals=q[idx]
maxv=vals.max()
if maxv <= 1e-300:
   return zeros,maxv
# weights: perhaps add small uniform over all top to avoid tie zero?
rel=vals/maxv
w = rel ** gamma
# If gamma large, some zero; add floor eps
w = w + 1e-12
w /= w.sum()
G=np.zeros(n)
# for speed, if k large? Loop
for ii in range(k):
   wi=float(w[ii]); ki=int(idx[ii])
   if wi<=0: continue
   js = ki - (n-1)
   if js <0: js=0
   je = ki
   if je >= n: je=n-1
   if js<=je:
      # p segment reversed
      G[js:je+1] += (2.0*wi) * p[js:je+1][::-1]
M = float(np.dot(q[idx], w))
return G,M
```

Adam step for logit:
```
def adam_logit_step(z,g, m,v,t,lr,beta1=0.9,beta2=0.999,eps=1e-8):
    t1=0.9**t; t2=0.999**t
    m[:] = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
    v[:] = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*g*g
    z = z - lr * (m/(1-t1)) / (np.sqrt(v/(1-t2))+eps)
    # renormalize z: subtract mean and clip
    z -= np.mean(z)
    z = np.clip(z, -50, 50)  # perhaps 50 enough
    return
```
But in-place modifying z? We can copy candidate z2 = z.copy(); then apply. Need not modify if reject. Use local.

```

Expand function:
```
def sequence_from_logW(logW,n,K):
   idx = np.floor(np.arange(n) * K / n).astype(np.int64)
   # To avoid under/overflow, w = exp(logW - max)
   w = np.exp(np.clip(logW - logW.max(), -50, 50))
   p = w[idx]
   s=p.sum()
   if s>0: p/=s
   else p=np.ones(n)/n
   return p, idx
```
Computing idx each call K small, floor n cost O(n). We can precompute idx once and pass. For optimize block, inside loop expand repeatedly. Precompute idx, counts. For w update: p = w[idx]/sum(w[idx]). Sum(w[idx]) = counts @ w /? Specifically counts = bincount idx. total = counts.dot(w). For K small, compute p = w[idx]; total = np.dot(counts,w); p/=total. This is O(n) for p plus K. Good.

Let's refine the weight/gradient details.
`weighted_gradient` returns G=∑w∂q = 2∑w p_{k-j}. M=weighted average q. For tangent: Gt=G - (p·G)1. But p·G = 2M if G as derivative of q. Let's verify with grad_c formula: G=∑w ∂q. p·G=∑p_j∑w2p_{k-j}=2∑w∑p_jp_{k-j}=2∑w q_k =2M. Good. So Gt=G-2M. Good.
If weights sum not exactly1 due floor, pG may not equal2M; use pG.

Random perturbation in optimizer:
```
def perturb_p(p, rng, scale):
   p2 = p + scale*rng.standard_normal(n)
   p2 = np.maximum(p2, 1e-15)
   if p2.sum()>0: return p2/p2.sum()
   else return p
```
Scale maybe 0.05. In log domain: z = log(p); z += noise*scale; p=softmax. For small p, noise. Use z.

Adam line search implementation details:
We need to avoid infinite loop if score not improving due to step too small. Use no_imp count; if noimp > patience, perturb and reset lr. If after many pert no improve, lower params.

`refine_full` returns `(best_score, best_p)`.
Pseudo:
```
def refine_full(p0,end_time,rng, topk=128,gamma=20.0,lr=0.1,patience=200):
 p=clip(normalize)
 n=len(p)
 z=np.log(p); z=z-z.mean()
 q=fast_conv(p); score=2*n*q.max()
 best=score; bestp=p.copy()
 beta1=.9; beta2=.999
 m=zeros; v=zeros; t=0
 cur_lr=lr
 noimp=0; cur_pat=patience
 while time.monotonic() < end_time:
   if noimp >= cur_pat:
      # random restart from best
      scale = 0.03 + 0.05*rng.random()
      z0=np.log(np.maximum(bestp,1e-300))-mean
      noise=scale*rng.standard_normal(n); noise-=noise.mean()
      z = z0+noise; z-=z.mean(); p=softmax(z)
      q=fast_conv(p); score=2*n*q.max()
      # reset m,v,t,lr
      m.fill(0);v.fill(0);t=0
      cur_lr=max(0.01,lr*0.7)
      noimp=0; cur_pat=max(50,cur_pat//2)
      continue
   G,M=weighted_gradient(p,q,n,topk,gamma)
   Gt=G - float(np.dot(p,G))
   norm = np.linalg.norm(Gt)
   if norm < 1e-14:
       noimp +=1; continue
   g = p * Gt
   # to make scale reasonable, maybe divide by sqrt(n)? But Adam self-normalizes.
   t +=1
   m2 = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
   v2 = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*g*g
   b1=1-beta1**t; b2=1-beta2**t
   step = cur_lr * (m2/b1)/(np.sqrt(v2/b2)+1e-8)
   step = np.clip(step, -10, 10) # avoid huge
   z2 = z - step
   z2 -= z2.mean()
   z2 = np.clip(z2, -50, 50)
   p2 = softmax(z2)
   # Ensure p2 valid
   q2 = fast_conv(p2); s2=2*n*q2.max()
   if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < score - 1e-14:
       # accept; maybe if s2 > best? no, < current
       m=m2; v=v2; z=z2; p=p2; q=q2; score=s2
       if s2 < best: best=s2; bestp=p2.copy(); noimp=0
       # adapt lr: if noimp<5 increase?
       cur_lr = min(0.5, cur_lr*1.02)
   else:
       # reject, reduce lr and try maybe smaller without changing m/v? We'll just reduce and continue
       cur_lr = max(1e-4, cur_lr*0.5)
       noimp +=1
       # if repeated rejects due to coarse, maybe try a smaller fixed step from p/z with no momentum
       # handled by noimp eventually
 return best,bestp
```
If step too large rejects, we waste; but noimp counts rejects. Good.
Potential issue: If gradient direction is good but initial lr too small, every step improves but slowly; noimp resets. Good.
If top gradient zero (norm small), noimp increments unrelated; patience triggers restart.

But if current p not best (due to previous non-improving micro), score variable should be current score; best separate. If a candidate s2 is worse than current but slightly better than best? We only accept if < current score, objective monotonically decreases current. If not, reject.

Could plateau due to max non-smooth; adding softmax gamma helps. If noimp high, restart.

`refine_block`:
```
def refine_block(n,K,logW0,end_time,rng,topk,gamma,lr,patience):
 idx,counts precomputed
 logW = logW0.copy()
 w=exp(norm), p=w[idx]; total=counts.dot(w); p/=total
 q,s...
 best
 m,v,t zeros K
 while time:
  if noimp>=patience: reset from best logW + noise etc
  G,M=weighted_gradient(p,q,...)
  Gt=G - dot(p,G)
  # Dlog = bincount(idx, weights=p*Gt) / counts
  D = np.bincount(idx, weights=p*Gt, minlength=K).astype(float) / np.maximum(counts,1)
  if norm D small...
  t++
  m2=...
  step = lr*(m2/b1)/(sqrt(v2/b2)+eps)
  logW2 = logW - step (clip changes maybe -10)
  logW2 -= logW2.max() # scale invariance; keep range
  w2=exp(logW2)
  p2=w2[idx]; total2=counts.dot(w2); p2/=total2
  q2,s2
  if s2 < score: accept, update logW=m/v else reject lr*0.5
 return best_score,p_best
```
The natural gradient D maybe average; dividing by counts makes scale independent of K. Good.
`logW2 -= max` after each update; w2. But if subtract max, the parameter is scale-invariant; okay. If we add noise, similar.

Block init logW scale arbitrary; normalize by max before expand.

`make_logW_init(K, rng, style)`:
```
def make_logW_init(K,rng,style):
  x=np.linspace(0,1,K)
  if style==0: logW = np.log(np.maximum(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(K)),1e-12))
  elif style==1: # exponential/ quadratic
      slope=rng.uniform(-2,2)
      curv=rng.uniform(-1,1)
      noise=rng.normal(0,0.3,K)
      logW=slope*x+curv*x*x+noise
  elif style==2: # sum of sinusoids in log domain
      W=np.ones(K)
      nf=rng.integers(1,4)
      for _ in range(nf):
          f=rng.uniform(0.5,3.0)
          a=rng.uniform(0.2,1.0)
          ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
          W += a*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*x+ph)
      W=np.maximum(W,0.05)
      logW=np.log(W)
  elif style==3: # two-block ramp
      split = max(2, K//4?) But for exact, set first half low second high.
      logW=np.empty(K); first=random fraction 0.2-0.5; values low/high with noise
  else random
  logW -= logW.max()
  return logW
```
But for block objective, linear W (positive) matters; log of W. For two-block optimum, choose split K/2 maybe low=2/3 high=4/3 if increasing. For general K, could use linear ramp 0.3..1.7? But many levels. Add a style specific K=2 optimum style not needed.

Also use parent downsampling as init: If parent available, downsample to K by block average max. `W = np.array([parent segment max/mean])`. logW.

Let's define functions for Fourier optimizer.
`generate_fourier_basis(n,M,rng,fmax)`:
```
f = rng.choice(np.arange(1, fmax+1), size=M, replace=False)
phase = rng.uniform(0,2π, M)
t = np.arange(n)/n
Z = np.empty((n,M))
# Perhaps mix sin and cos: use two modes per frequency? If M even, unique freqs for sin/cos.
for col in range(M):
   # types sin/cos
   Z[:,col] = np.sin(2π f col t + phase)
# scale columns maybe 1/sqrt(M)
```
Could include constant? softmax constant no effect. Frequencies up to n/2. Random high frequency may produce noisy p; okay.
Alternatively use a fixed sequence: first half sin frequencies, second cos. For M=64, freqs 1..32 sin/cos. This gives smooth basis. Random phase. This may be better. For higher, up to M/2 maybe.

But if frequencies too high, p oscillatory nonneg via softmax may have zeros/near; allowed. Could help flatten.

Fourier optimizer `refine_subspace_basis(Z, end_time, rng, topk, gamma, lr, patience)`:
- M=Z.shape[1]
- c = rng.normal(0, 1.0/np.sqrt(M), M) maybe; z=Z@c; p=softmax(z)
- q,score best.
- Adam arrays M.
- Iterate:
   G, Mq = weighted_gradient(p,q,...)
   gz = p*(G - dot(p,G))
   gc = Z.T @ gz  # M
   # If gc small, maybe add random c perturb.
   Adam step lr onto c.
   z2=Z@c2; p2=softmax(z2)
   q2 score; accept if improve.
- Restart if no improve: c = best_c + noise*scale, reset Adam, lr lower.
Since Z matrix defines map, p always in subspace. The gradient gc is exact projection of logit gradient.

def gen_basis(n,M,rng, max_freq):
    # use frequencies 1..ceil(max_freq) with sin/cos; choose first M combos
    fmax = max(2, int(0.15*n))? For M=128, freqs up to perhaps 256? Higher frequencies okay. Let use frequencies from 1 to min(n//2, 4*M) unique.
    t = np.arange(n) / float(n)
    Z = np.empty((n,M))
    freqs=[]
    for col in range(M):
       # Use col//2+1 frequency, sin/cos
       f = (col//2)+1
       if f > n//2: f = n//2
       ph = rng.uniform(0, 2*np.pi)
       if col%2==0: Z[:,col]=np.sin(2*np.pi*f*t + ph)
       else: Z[:,col]=np.cos(2*np.pi*f*t + ph)
    # Standardize columns: subtract mean, divide std
    means=Z.mean(axis=0); stds=Z.std(axis=0); stds[stds<1e-8]=1
    Z=(Z-means)/stds/np.sqrt(M)? Actually we can leave unit RMS; Adam adapts. Maybe scale by 1 to not too large. Use Z/=np.sqrt(M) to keep z variance ~1 if c~1.
```

def refine_fourier(n, Z, end_time, rng, topk=128,gamma=15,lr=0.05,patience=100):
 c = rng.normal(0,0.3,size=M)
 z=Z@c; p=softmax(z)
 best...
 m_c,v_c,t
 while time<end:
   if noimp... restart c = best_c + 0.2*rng.normal, reset
   G,_=weighted_gradient
   gz=p*(G-dot(p,G))
   gc=Z.T.dot(gz)
   if norm(gc)<1e-12: noimp++ continue
   Adam step c2
   p2=softmax(Z@c2)
   q2,s2
   if s2<score: accept
```

`fast_self_conv(a)`:
```
def fast_self_conv(a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 1:
       return np.array([a[0]*a[0]])
    m = 2*n -1
    N = 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)[:m]
    # Remove tiny imaginary? irfft real.
    # Due to roundoff, values can be -1e-20; set negative to 0 if small? But if q legitimate zero, max unaffected. Use np.maximum(c,0) maybe changes gradient slightly. Use c = np.where(c>0,c,0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
Clipping negative to 0 might make sum not exactly1 but fine.

`normalize_p(p,n)`:
```
def normalize_p(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
    p=np.where(np.isfinite(p),p,0.0)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<1e-300:
       return np.ones(p.size)/p.size
    return p/s
```
`logit_to_p(z)`:
```
def logit_to_p(z):
   z=np.where(np.isfinite(z),z,0.0)
   z=z-np.max(z)
   z=np.clip(z,-50,50)
   p=np.exp(z)
   s=p.sum()
   if s<1e-300: return uniform
   return p/s
```
For sequence from basis, z maybe all finite.

`weighted_gradient(p,q,n,k,gamma)`:
Topk selection: if k>q.size, k=q.size. If k==0? use1.
```
if k == 1:
   idx = np.argmax(q)
   vals=q[idx]
else:
   idx = np.argpartition(q, -k)[-k:]
   vals=q[idx]
maxv = vals.max()
if maxv <= 1e-300:
   w = np.full(n, 1.0/n)? But q zero impossible. Return zeros.
rel = np.maximum(vals,0.0)/maxv
# w = rel**gamma; if gamma large underflow floor
w = np.power(rel, gamma) if gamma>0 else np.ones(k)
floor = 1e-12
w = w + floor
w /= w.sum()
G = np.zeros(n)
# optional: sort by weight? no.
for t in range(k):
   wi = w[t]
   if wi <= 0: continue
   ki = int(idx[t])
   js = ki - (n-1)
   if js <0: js=0
   je = ki
   if je > n-1: je = n-1
   if js <= je:
      G[js:je+1] += (2.0*wi) * p[js:je+1][::-1]
M = float(np.dot(q[idx], w))  # weighted maybe normalized weights
return G,M
```
Using rel = vals/maxv; if vals near max, weights for lower q maybe very small if gamma high. Floor 1e-12 okay. If gamma=50, values 0.9^50=0.005; 0.5^50=1e-15 floor. Good.

But if q values are all comparable (flat), rel all~1, weights uniform over k. Good.

In `refine_full`, we need to copy end_time float. Use `while time.monotonic() < end_time:`. But if end_time passed as `start + total*...`, okay.

Restart noise in full refine: We need z from bestp. `z0 = np.log(np.maximum(bestp, 1e-300))`; `z0 -= z0.mean()`. `noise = rng.standard_normal(n); noise -= noise.mean(); z = z0 + (0.05 + 0.1*rng.random())*noise`. Normalize z.

In Adam step, use local m2/v2:
```
b1 = 1.0 - beta1 ** t
b2 = 1.0 - beta2 ** t
m2 = beta1 * m + (1.0 - beta1) * g
v2 = beta2 * v + (1.0 - beta2) * g * g
step = cur_lr * (m2 / b1) / (np.sqrt(v2 / b2) + 1e-8)
step = np.clip(step, -8.0, 8.0)
z2 = z - step
```
Should we use `m2/b1` or bias-corrected? Yes.

If accepted, set m=m2,v=v2,t=t. If rejected, do not update. If rejected many, noimp++.
But if cur_lr reduced below min, maybe do a random perturbation to attempt. noimp will trigger after patience.

Weighted gradient may produce G with NaN if q NaN. Check at loop start if not finite score, restart.
```
if not np.isfinite(score): p=bestp; z...; q=...
```
At end return best_score,best_p.

`refine_fourier`:
```
def refine_fourier(n,Z,end_time,rng,topk,gamma,lr,patience,max_lr,min_lr):
 M=Z.shape[1]
 c = rng.normal(0,0.3,M)
 z=Z.dot(c); p=logit_to_p(z)
 q,score
 best_c=c.copy()
 m_c=np.zeros(M); v_c=zeros; t=0
 cur_lr=lr; noimp=0; cur_pat=patience
 while time<end:
   if noimp>=cur_pat:
      scale=0.1+0.2*rng.random()
      c=best_c + scale*rng.normal(0,1,M)
      z=Z.dot(c); p=logit_to_p(z); q,score...
      m_c.fill(0); v_c.fill(0); t=0; cur_lr=max(min_lr,lr*0.6); noimp=0; cur_pat=max(50,cur_pat//2); continue
   G,Mq=weighted_gradient(p,q,n,topk,gamma)
   gz=p*(G-float(np.dot(p,G)))
   gc=Z.T.dot(gz)
   if norm(gc)<1e-14: noimp++; continue
   t+=1
   c2 = c.copy(); m2=...; step=...; c2 -= step; c2 = np.clip(c2,-20,20)
   p2=logit_to_p(Z.dot(c2)); q2,s2
   if s2<score-1e-15: accept; best_c=c2.copy(); ... else reject
 return score,p
```
Should we add a constant offset to c? z constant no effect. No.
`Z.T.dot(gz)` length M; ensure float.
`c2` clip to avoid huge z.

Basis generation:
```
def generate_basis(n,M,rng):
    t = np.arange(n)/float(n)
    Z = np.empty((n,M), dtype=np.float64)
    # use M modes: frequency col//2 +1, sin/cos with random phase
    for col in range(M):
       f = (col // 2) + 1
       if f > n//2: f = n//2
       ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi)
       if col % 2 == 0:
          Z[:,col] = np.sin(2.0*np.pi*f*t + ph)
       else:
          Z[:,col] = np.cos(2.0*np.pi*f*t + ph)
    # center and scale
    means = Z.mean(axis=0)
    stds = Z.std(axis=0)
    stds[stds < 1e-8] = 1.0
    Z = (Z - means) / stds
    Z = Z / np.sqrt(float(M))
    return Z
```
For M=128, freq up to64; okay. Could include frequencies up to larger by random selection: Maybe use random frequencies for latter modes to increase diversity. Use first M/2 low, rest random? Simpler: f = max(1, int(rng.uniform(1, min(n//2, 4*(col//2+1)))))? But deterministic low frequencies enough. Random phase gives different shapes.
Maybe for multiple restarts, regenerate basis and c. Good.

```
def exact_score_list(a_list):
    a=np.asarray(a_list,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    b=np.convolve(a,a)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    return float(2*n*np.max(b)/(s*s))
```

Let's verify the gradient formula orientation one more time with an example to avoid wrong direction. n=3, p=[a,b,c]. q0=a^2, q1=2ab, q2=2ac+b^2, q3=2bc,c4=c^2.
For active k=2 (center-ish), derivative ∂q2: ∂/a=2c, ∂/b=2b, ∂/c=2a. Our loop: ki=2, js=0,je=2, G[0:3] +=2w p[0:3][::-1] = [2c,2b,2a]. Good.

Gt = G - p·G. For k=2 uniform: p=1/3, G=[2/3,2/3,2/3], pG=2/3? actually sum p*G = 3*(1/3*2/3)=2/3. Gt=0. Uniform stationary for center. If choose k=3, G=[0,2/3,2/3], pG=4/9? Gt=[-4/9,2/9,2/9]; update moves mass? gz=p*Gt. This can reduce q3 by shifting mass from b/c? Maybe. Good.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2

Strategy:
- Work on the probability simplex (sum = 1); the objective is scale invariant.
- Use fast FFT self-convolution.
- Use a soft active-set gradient: take the largest convolution coefficients,
  weight them smoothly, and form the corresponding quadratic gradient.
- Optimize in log-softmax coordinates with Adam-like adaptive steps and restarts.
- Additionally run lower-dimensional Fourier-subspace searches to produce
  diverse, non-trivial starting points and then polish in the full space.
- Return the best sequence found before the deadline.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def pow2_ge(n):
    if n <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (n - 1).bit_length()


def fast_self_conv(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a via real FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([float(a[0] * a[0])], dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    N = pow2_ge(m)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)[:m]
    # Remove tiny negative values caused by floating point roundoff.
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def score_from_q(q, n):
    if n <= 0 or q.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    mx = float(np.max(q))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mx)


def normalize_p(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, p.size))
    return p / s


def logit_to_p(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 50.0)
    p = np.exp(z)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, p.size))
    return p / s


def weighted_gradient(p, q, n, topk, gamma):
    """
    Gradient of a smooth surrogate for max(q), restricted to top peaks.
    Returns (G, M) where G = sum_i w_i * grad(q_i), and M = sum_i w_i q_i.
    """
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    L = q.size
    if L == 0 or n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64), 0.0
    topk = int(max(1, min(topk, L)))
    if topk == 1:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(q))], dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(q, -topk)[-topk:]
        idx = idx.astype(np.int64)
    vals = q[idx]
    maxv = float(np.max(vals)) if vals.size else 0.0
    if maxv <= 1e-300:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64), 0.0

    rel = np.maximum(vals, 0.0) / maxv
    if gamma > 0.0:
        w = np.power(rel, float(gamma))
    else:
        w = np.ones_like(rel)
    w = w + 1e-12
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.full_like(w, 1.0 / w.size)
    else:
        w = w / s

    G = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for ii in range(idx.size):
        wi = float(w[ii])
        if wi <= 0.0:
            continue
        ki = int(idx[ii])
        js = ki - (n - 1)
        if js < 0:
            js = 0
        je = ki
        if je >= n:
            je = n - 1
        if js <= je:
            G[js:je + 1] += (2.0 * wi) * p[js:je + 1][::-1]
    M = float(np.dot(q[idx], w))
    return G, M


def refine_full(p0, end_time, rng, topk, gamma, lr, patience):
    """
    Full-space optimizer in log-softmax coordinates with Adam-like updates.
    """
    p = normalize_p(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.inf, p

    z = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300), dtype=np.float64)
    z -= float(np.mean(z))
    p = logit_to_p(z)

    q = fast_self_conv(p)
    score = score_from_q(q, n)
    best_score = score
    best_p = p.copy()

    beta1 = 0.9
    beta2 = 0.999
    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    cur_lr = float(lr)
    max_lr = 0.35
    min_lr = 1e-5
    no_imp = 0
    cur_pat = int(max(30, patience))

    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            p = normalize_p(best_p)
            z = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
            z -= float(np.mean(z))
            p = logit_to_p(z)
            q = fast_self_conv(p)
            score = score_from_q(q, n)
            m[:] = 0.0
            v[:] = 0.0
            t = 0
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, 0.7 * cur_lr)
            no_imp = 0
            continue

        if no_imp >= cur_pat:
            # Restart from best with logit noise.
            scale = 0.05 + 0.15 * float(rng.random())
            z0 = np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-300), dtype=np.float64)
            z0 -= float(np.mean(z0))
            noise = rng.standard_normal(n)
            noise -= float(np.mean(noise))
            z = z0 + scale * noise
            p = logit_to_p(z)
            q = fast_self_conv(p)
            score = score_from_q(q, n)
            m[:] = 0.0
            v[:] = 0.0
            t = 0
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, 0.65 * float(lr))
            no_imp = 0
            cur_pat = max(40, cur_pat // 2)
            continue

        G, _ = weighted_gradient(p, q, n, topk, gamma)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(G)):
            no_imp += 1
            continue
        pG = float(np.dot(p, G))
        Gt = G - pG
        nrm2 = float(np.dot(Gt, Gt))
        if nrm2 < 1e-28:
            no_imp += 1
            continue

        g = p * Gt
        t += 1
        bias1 = 1.0 - beta1 ** t
        bias2 = 1.0 - beta2 ** t
        m2 = beta1 * m + (1.0 - beta1) * g
        v2 = beta2 * v + (1.0 - beta2) * g * g
        step = cur_lr * (m2 / bias1) / (np.sqrt(v2 / bias2) + 1e-8)
        step = np.clip(step, -12.0, 12.0)
        z2 = z - step
        z2 -= float(np.mean(z2))
        z2 = np.clip(z2, -50.0, 50.0)
        p2 = logit_to_p(z2)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p2)):
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, 0.5 * cur_lr)
            no_imp += 1
            continue
        q2 = fast_self_conv(p2)
        s2 = score_from_q(q2, n)

        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < score:
            z = z2
            p = p2
            q = q2
            score = s2
            m = m2
            v = v2
            if score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_p = p.copy()
                no_imp = 0
            if no_imp % 20 == 0:
                cur_lr = min(max_lr, cur_lr * 1.02)
        else:
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, cur_lr * 0.55)
            no_imp += 1

    if best_score < score:
        return best_score, best_p.copy()
    return score, p.copy()


def generate_basis(n, M, rng):
    """Random Fourier basis: mostly low-frequency sine/cosine combinations."""
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n))
    Z = np.empty((n, M), dtype=np.float64)
    max_allowed = max(1, n // 2)
    for col in range(M):
        f = (col // 2) + 1
        if f > max_allowed:
            f = max_allowed
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        if (col % 2) == 0:
            Z[:, col] = np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * x + ph)
        else:
            Z[:, col] = np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * x + ph)
    mn = Z.mean(axis=0)
    sd = Z.std(axis=0)
    bad = sd < 1e-8
    if np.any(bad):
        sd[bad] = 1.0
    Z = (Z - mn) / sd
    Z = Z / np.sqrt(float(M))
    return Z


def refine_fourier(n, Z, end_time, rng, topk, gamma, lr, patience):
    """
    Optimize p = softmax(Z @ c) in a fixed Fourier subspace.
    """
    M = Z.shape[1]
    if M == 0:
        p0 = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        q0 = fast_self_conv(p0)
        return score_from_q(q0, n), p0

    c = rng.normal(0.0, 0.35, size=M)
    z = Z.dot(c)
    p = logit_to_p(z)
    q = fast_self_conv(p)
    score = score_from_q(q, n)
    best_score = score
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_c = c.copy()

    beta1 = 0.9
    beta2 = 0.999
    mc = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
    vc = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    cur_lr = float(lr)
    max_lr = 0.5
    min_lr = 1e-5
    no_imp = 0
    cur_pat = int(max(30, patience))

    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            c = best_c.copy()
            p = logit_to_p(Z.dot(c))
            q = fast_self_conv(p)
            score = score_from_q(q, n)
            mc[:] = 0.0
            vc[:] = 0.0
            t = 0
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, 0.7 * cur_lr)
            no_imp = 0
            continue

        if no_imp >= cur_pat:
            scale = 0.12 + 0.25 * float(rng.random())
            c = best_c + scale * rng.standard_normal(M)
            p = logit_to_p(Z.dot(c))
            q = fast_self_conv(p)
            score = score_from_q(q, n)
            mc[:] = 0.0
            vc[:] = 0.0
            t = 0
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, 0.65 * float(lr))
            no_imp = 0
            cur_pat = max(30, cur_pat // 2)
            continue

        G, _ = weighted_gradient(p, q, n, topk, gamma)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(G)):
            no_imp += 1
            continue
        pG = float(np.dot(p, G))
        gz = p * (G - pG)
        gc = Z.T.dot(gz)
        nrm2 = float(np.dot(gc, gc))
        if nrm2 < 1e-28:
            no_imp += 1
            continue

        t += 1
        bias1 = 1.0 - beta1 ** t
        bias2 = 1.0 - beta2 ** t
        m2 = beta1 * mc + (1.0 - beta1) * gc
        v2 = beta2 * vc + (1.0 - beta2) * gc * gc
        step = cur_lr * (m2 / bias1) / (np.sqrt(v2 / bias2) + 1e-8)
        step = np.clip(step, -8.0, 8.0)
        c2 = c - step
        c2 = np.clip(c2, -25.0, 25.0)
        p2 = logit_to_p(Z.dot(c2))
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p2)):
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, 0.5 * cur_lr)
            no_imp += 1
            continue
        q2 = fast_self_conv(p2)
        s2 = score_from_q(q2, n)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < score:
            c = c2
            p = p2
            q = q2
            score = s2
            mc = m2
            vc = v2
            if score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_p = p.copy()
                best_c = c.copy()
                no_imp = 0
            if no_imp % 20 == 0:
                cur_lr = min(max_lr, cur_lr * 1.02)
        else:
            cur_lr = max(min_lr, cur_lr * 0.55)
            no_imp += 1

    if best_score < score:
        return best_score, best_p.copy()
    return score, p.copy()


def make_init(n, kind, rng):
    """Create one diverse nonnegative initialization."""
    if kind == 0:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    elif kind == 1:
        a = np.exp(0.55 * rng.standard_normal(n))
        a = np.maximum(a, 1e-3)
    elif kind == 2:
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        a = 1.0 + 0.95 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x + ph)
        a = np.maximum(a, 1e-3)
    elif kind == 3:
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        hi = max(2, min(20, n // 4))
        f1 = int(rng.integers(1, hi + 1))
        f2 = int(rng.integers(1, hi + 1))
        p1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        p2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        a = (1.0
             + 0.55 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f1) * x + p1)
             + 0.35 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f2) * x + p2))
        a = np.maximum(a, 1e-3)
    elif kind == 4:
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        slope = float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0))
        a = np.exp(slope * x + 0.2 * rng.standard_normal(n))
        a = np.maximum(a, 1e-3)
    elif kind == 5:
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        hi = max(2, min(12, n // 4))
        f = int(rng.integers(1, hi + 1))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        a = 1.0 + 0.85 * np.sign(np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * x + ph))
        a = np.where(a > 1e-3, a, 1e-3)
    else:
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        a = 0.35 + np.sin(np.pi * x) ** 2 + 0.15 * rng.standard_normal(n)
        a = np.maximum(a, 1e-3)
    return normalize_p(a)


def exact_score_array(a):
    """Exact score using direct convolution, for final verification only."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    b = np.convolve(a, a)
    mx = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mx / (s * s))


def pick_topk(n, denominator, lo=32, hi=1200):
    v = int(n // max(1, denominator))
    return int(max(lo, min(hi, v)))


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """Find and return a sequence minimizing the given objective."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    start = time.monotonic()
    total = float(budget_s) - 20.0
    if total < 120.0:
        total = max(120.0, float(budget_s) / 2.0)
    deadline = start + total

    # Determine working length. Prefer the provided parent if sane.
    n = 8192
    parent = None
    try:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if arr.size >= 512 and arr.size <= 12288 and arr.sum() > 1e-8:
                n = int(arr.size)
                parent = normalize_p(arr)
            else:
                parent = None
        except Exception:
            parent = None

    if parent is not None and parent.size == n:
        best_p = parent.copy()
    else:
        best_p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    q_best = fast_self_conv(best_p)
    best_score = score_from_q(q_best, n)
    print(f"n={n} parent={'yes' if parent is not None else 'no'} start={best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # 1) Refine parent if present.
    t = start
    if parent is not None:
        end1 = min(deadline, t + min(320.0, 0.12 * total))
        s, p = refine_full(
            parent, end1, rng,
            topk=pick_topk(n, 32, 48, 256),
            gamma=10.0,
            lr=0.05,
            patience=180
        )
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
            best_score = s
            best_p = p.copy()
        print(f"after parent refine: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
        t = min(deadline, time.monotonic())

    # 2) Fourier-subspace global searches.
    t_fourier_end = min(deadline, t + 0.34 * total)
    fourier_configs = [
        (16, 2),
        (32, 3),
        (64, 3),
        (128, 2),
    ]
    jobs = []
    for M, reps in fourier_configs:
        for _ in range(reps):
            jobs.append(M)
    if len(jobs) > 0 and t_fourier_end > t:
        per_job = max(5.0, (t_fourier_end - t) / float(len(jobs)))
        for M in jobs:
            if time.monotonic() >= t_fourier_end:
                break
            job_end = min(t_fourier_end, time.monotonic() + per_job)
            Z = generate_basis(n, int(M), rng)
            s, p = refine_fourier(
                n, Z, job_end, rng,
                topk=pick_topk(n, 16, 64, 384),
                gamma=12.0,
                lr=0.07,
                patience=90
            )
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
                best_score = s
                best_p = p.copy()
                print(f"fourier M={M}: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
        t = min(deadline, time.monotonic())

    # 3) Direct random full-space restarts.
    t_random_end = min(deadline, t + 0.26 * total)
    if t_random_end > t:
        random_kinds = [1, 3, 4, 5, 6]
        per_job = max(8.0, (t_random_end - t) / float(len(random_kinds)))
        for kind in random_kinds:
            if time.monotonic() >= t_random_end:
                break
            p0 = make_init(n, int(kind), rng)
            job_end = min(t_random_end, time.monotonic() + per_job)
            s, p = refine_full(
                p0, job_end, rng,
                topk=pick_topk(n, 20, 64, 384),
                gamma=12.0,
                lr=0.06,
                patience=120
            )
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
                best_score = s
                best_p = p.copy()
                print(f"random init {kind}: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
        t = min(deadline, time.monotonic())

    # 4) Final staged full-space polish.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 60.0:
        mid_end = time.monotonic() + 0.55 * rem
        s, p = refine_full(
            best_p, mid_end, rng,
            topk=pick_topk(n, 12, 96, 512),
            gamma=14.0,
            lr=0.05,
            patience=350
        )
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
            best_score = s
            best_p = p.copy()
        print(f"final phase 1: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    rem2 = deadline - 5.0 - time.monotonic()
    if rem2 > 30.0:
        s, p = refine_full(
            best_p, deadline - 5.0, rng,
            topk=pick_topk(n, 8, 256, 1024),
            gamma=35.0,
            lr=0.018,
            patience=600
        )
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
            best_score = s
            best_p = p.copy()
        print(f"final phase 2: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Clean and scale output safely.
    out = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if out.sum() <= 1e-12:
        out = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    mx = float(np.max(out))
    if mx > 1e-300 and np.isfinite(mx):
        out = out / mx
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1.0)

    # Optional final verification, but do not let it crash the solution.
    try:
        miss = exact_score_array(out)
        print(f"FINAL internal={best_score:.8f} exact_scaled={miss:.8f} n={n} "
              f"time={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s", flush=True)
    except Exception:
        print(f"FINAL internal={best_score:.8f} exact_scaled=ERROR n={n}", flush=True)

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
